60. **The Explorer Quiz** (Simon, 2026-10-16: a fun feature on the Explorer's page, beside How to read the Explorer and in its style: one unique question at a time, made from the chain, with a field for the answer that is checked, at the bottom of the screen while the Explorer is explored for the answer; interesting, sometimes tricky and clever questions that need digging).
    - **The button** "Explorer Quiz" (outlined, beside How to read the Explorer) makes a question and opens the bar; pressed with a question open, it just opens the bar.
    - **The bar** (`components/explorer/quiz/QuizDock.tsx`, loaded only while the quiz is on, mounted by AppShell on every Explorer page) sits at the bottom of the screen, above the phone's bottom bar, its height kept clear for focus: the question (names in bold, an address whole, blocks with their number), its level (Easy green, Medium blue, Tricky violet), the score ("3 of 5 right · 2 in a row"), the answer field and Check. A wrong answer: "Not quite. Look again, or take a hint." Hint says where to look without giving it away; Show the answer gives it with how it is found and a link to the page that shows it; Right! the same, then Next question takes the focus. Another question, and a Level picker (any, Easy, Medium, Tricky). Folds to one line; × closes it, keeping the score.
    - **The questions** (`data/explorer/quiz/generators.ts`, 25 templates): easy (a member's level, the block it was first seen in, an address's primary name, how many names or groups it has, how much one account paid another in a block), medium (who removed an account from a group in a block, how many transactions a block holds without messages, how many names were registered in 100 blocks, who sent an account its first QORT, who sponsored it, what a name sold for and who sold it, how many of its groups it runs, what a name was renamed to, who created a group), tricky (the nth self share in a stretch of blocks, the sponsor's sponsor, where an account's minting level came from, the type of the first transaction it made itself, how many payments a multi-payment held, how much QORT or how many payments one account ever sent another, the first 8 characters of the newest AT transaction that paid an account, who got the biggest payment in a block, how many "Joining a group" transactions a group had in 1,000 blocks). Each picks at random, from MINTER's members or from a random stretch of blocks searched for one type, and reads the answer the way the Explorer shows it (mostly the Explorer's own cached reads); one that finds nothing fair gives way to another (10 tries; "Couldn't make a question just now. Your node may be busy." with Try again). None is asked twice on a device (the newest 300 keys kept), nor the same template twice in a row. Levels come up 35 % easy, 40 % medium, 25 % tricky unless one is picked.
    - **Checking** (`rules/explorer/quiz/check.ts`): an account by its address, the name it was asked by, or any name it owns (looked up through Core, which ignores case and look-alike letters); numbers with or without commas, "QORT", "#" or "block", whole numbers exact, amounts rounded to 2 decimals or more accepted; words ignoring case and punctuation ("Name registration" or REGISTER_NAME); a signature by its first characters.
    - **Kept on the device** (`<prefix>explorer-quiz`): the question and how it stands, so it survives moving between Explorer pages and a reload. Read-only like the rest of the Explorer: it only reads (all GET), and only when asked.
    Checked on mainnet, every template once: "In block 2,108,885, ISFR was removed from the group “MINTER” (#694). Who removed it?" (Qrowdydowdys1), "Which account made the tenth self share … in the 1,000 blocks from block 641,761?" (Peach), "QUgPPE1e… got its minting level from another account. Which one?" (HugeOven). New screenshot screens explorer-quiz and explorer-quiz-answered (phones show the score short, "3/5 right"); 14 new tests for the quiz, 12 for items 55–59; tests 2,152 passed.

61. **The Explorer Quiz, better questions** (Simon, 2026-10-17: think the questions and their making over, so they are better, trickier, never repeating, sometimes harder, better built, more unique, smart and sometimes funny).
    - **Four levels**: Easy, Medium, Tricky and a new **Expert** (several hops, sums, deep history). Unless one is picked, the level follows the player: about a quarter easy, a third medium, a quarter tricky, the rest expert; each right answer in a row leans harder (up to 6), an answer shown leans easier, and the first two questions are never expert (`rules/explorer/quiz/pick.ts`).
    - **Never the same thing twice in a row**: no kind of question among the last 3 asked, kinds asked lately come up less, a theme other than the last one's is favoured, and the accounts, names, blocks and groups of recent questions (the newest 60) are left alone for a while; a question itself never comes back (its key).
    - **59 kinds in 12 themes**, each with a heading over the question ("Follow the money", "Group drama", "Minting archaeology", "Chain history", "Detective work", "Head to head", "Odd one out", "The people have spoken"…) and two or three wordings, some playful ("Who swung the ban hammer on … in …?", "Sponsor-ception: who sponsored the account that sponsored …?", "… was shown the door of …. Who held it open?", "Unmask this address"). New: chain history (the very first name, payment, group, QDN publish, poll, vote, name sale, listing, rename, level move, AT, removal and ban on Qortal, each asked once, with the 10,000 blocks it is in; block 1's assets; block 2's 19 self shares), multiple choice with one go (higher or lower, spot the Minter Admin, odd one out of MINTER, who owns the most names, who is in the most groups, who was first seen earliest), the day of the week a name was registered, what a block's payments add up to, which type a block has most of, how many accounts made a block, who sent a block's biggest payment, who an account paid most, who bought, sold, listed or renamed a name, who registered a name that changed hands since, who banned or promoted whom, which group someone left, polls (who asked, which option a vote picked, how many options), a trade contract's payout and its signature, a sponsor's sponsor, the sponsor of the account a level came from, the level of whoever removed someone, the first QORT of the first QORT's sender, how long a reward share ran, how many accounts a sponsor sponsored, the nth self share deep in a stretch. Sponsors are now read as Connections reads them (its links and rules: "first" is the first one listed), not Core's arbitrary pick.
    - **Better built**: every answer is read the way the Explorer shows it (its own cached reads mostly), anchored to a block where it can be ("in block …"), and questions that would be ambiguous are skipped (two payments between the same accounts in one block, a tie for the biggest, two sponsors in one block, a member named like its group, a buyer named like the name it bought, an owner known by the very name asked about, a poll whose question names its own answer, polls an app made or encrypted). Live answers (a level, how many names or groups, totals between two accounts, how many accounts a sponsor sponsored) are read again when an answer doesn't match, so a question asked yesterday is still fair today.
    - **Smart help**: Hint says where to look; Closer hint narrows it (sometimes with the 10,000 blocks to search) and offers "Show me where", the page that shows it. The answer comes with how it is found and a fact (the date, "1,176 days ago", the four first-seen blocks of a head to head, a poll's options).
    - **And fun**: varied words for a right answer ("Spot on!", "Correct. The chain agrees.", "Right, detective."), a miss ("The chain says otherwise. Try again?"), a missed pick ("So close. Or not."), streaks ("Three in a row!", "Ten in a row. Are you a node?"), and while it thinks ("Asking your node nicely…", "Digging through blocks…"). Mainnet supplies some of its own: the first poll on Qortal asked "Are puppy ears soft?", with three options that all say yes.
    Checked on mainnet: every kind made questions (most in under 0.2 s; chain history about 2 s the first time); a run of 15 questions in a row gave 15 kinds across every theme and level. Q-Gram's encrypted polls and app-made rating polls are left out. 17 new tests (the picker, multiple choice, live answers, two-step hints, old saved quiz state, chain history on the mock world); tests 2,169 passed; new screen explorer-quiz-choice.

64. **Open File and Save File** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "the ability for explorer to also be able to open and view the files in explorer (all kinds of files that were published to QDN), after clicking the button "Open File" (Torq has quite a bit of ability to view all kinds of files, Q-Mail and Q-Share too)"; then "and of course Download too", "call Download button: Save File"). A public QDN publish (never an encrypted one, nor one without a name) has **Open File** and **Save File** beside Open transaction in its row's details, and under its sentence on its own page. Nothing is read before a press (`components/explorer/file`, `data/explorer/qdnFile.ts`, `rules/explorer/qdnFiles.ts`).
    - **The viewer** (its own 50 KB chunk; full screen on phones and in a frame under 500 px tall, where only the line of who, what and how big stays above the file; tall on wider screens): the file's name as its title, the publisher's title and description, who published it, the service, size and kind in words, then the file. While the node gets it: "Getting the file to your node…", its parts and a bar ("1 of 2 parts (50%)"), a stall said after 90 s; a file of 50 MB or more asks first ("Get it from the network?", with what it costs). Core's final statuses are said in words (blocked, unsupported, couldn't be put together, not listed). When the transaction published an earlier version, it says so: Core serves the latest, with a link to that version's transaction.
    - **Its kind** from the publisher's metadata (type, file name), Core's type, the service and the file's first bytes, which win where certain (`resolveFileKind`: a PDF published as a DOCUMENT is a PDF; a DOCX is named, not listed as the ZIP it is inside).
    - **Shown:** pictures with zoom, pinch and pan (Q-Mail+'s viewer); video and sound streamed from the node (Core serves ranges); PDFs page by page with zoom and the dark page view, through pdf.js 4.10 set up as Q-Mail+ does in Hub (worker on the main thread; its own 1.7 MB chunk, 506 KB gzipped, loaded with the first PDF; up to 100 MB); JSON laid out in the Explorer's colours (the raw response's view); CSV and TSV as a table (500 rows, Show as text); text and HTML source (512 KB, text size; HTML never runs, SVG only through `<img>`); a ZIP's files from its central directory (names, sizes, dates, folders, password marks; nothing inside opened); anything else named in words with its first bytes in hex. A website's or app's files are listed (filter, 100 at a time) and open one by one; **Open in Qortal** opens an app or a website published under its default identifier (`OPEN_NEW_TAB`).
    - **Save File** goes through Hub's save prompt (`SAVE_FILE`), as Q-Share+ does it (docs/QORTAL.md pitfall 17): a blob on desktop Hub under 100 MB; by `location` in GO and for bigger files (Hub streams it from the node); a file inside a resource of several as a blob; a "no" in Hub's prompt is quiet. Pressed in a row or on the page, it opens the viewer and saves once the file is on the node. Outside Hub it is a plain download link.
    - **Reads:** see Explorer reads. Core doesn't hold a byte range's end to the file's size (a longer one breaks the answer), so every range after the first one-byte read is cut to the size.
    - **Checked with real data** in the browser against Simon's node (dev server, signed out): Juan Qortal's Q-Share PDF (5 pages, dark view), a Torq comment (JSON), Q-Mintership+'s own app (116 files listed, favicon.png opened), igorcoin's image (the node had 1 of 2 parts and no peer offering the other: the viewer kept waiting and said so). The mock world has two files (Avery Admin's JSON settings, AA Maps' website) and two new screens, explorer-file and explorer-file-list.
    - **Tests:** the file kinds and their signatures, ZIP listings (ZIP64 too), tables, hex; the reads (paths with spaces and `+`, metadata none, statuses, the latest version, ranges cut to the size); Save File's choices; the viewer (nothing before a press, waiting then the file, a big file asked first, a blocked one, a website's files, an earlier version, Save File from outside, an encrypted publish without either button), with axe.
    - **Bundle:** dist 4.2 MB (2.5 MB before), the zip 1.6 MB (1.1 MB): pdf.js is 1.7 MB of it, loaded only with a PDF.
    - A resource's only file kept in a folder is read by its path (Core picks an only file at the top only).

65. **A search on an account's Connections and Transactions** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "smaller search bars for connections and transactions for each individual account (so users can search these too, and my typing something not fully I want it to also find similar)"). A small field at the top of each tab ("Search these connections", "Search these transactions"; 340 px from 600 px, the whole width on phones; Clear and Escape empty it; `?q=` in the link) narrows what the tab has read to the rows that show what was typed (`utils/fuzzySearch.ts`, `rules/explorer/searchText.ts`, `components/explorer/search`).
    - **What counts:** what a row shows (its sentence, amounts, the type and its family, tags such as MINTER member or "its sponsor", the block, the date, the signature), the names known for its accounts and their addresses; also a name an account holds besides the one shown, through Core's name search ("holds the name “Simon Dixon”" says why such a row is there). Never what a row hides: a message's content or an encrypted resource's identifier can't be searched.
    - **Typed in part, and similar:** any case, accents and look-alike forms, invisible characters ignored (a name with the blank Braille character is found by its letters), digit groups joined (1,371,641 or 1371641); every word typed must be found. From 4 letters a word one letter off (two from 8), typed only in part with a slip, or its letters in order is **similar**: such rows come after the exact matches, under "Similar to “…” not exact: a letter or two differ". Numbers match from a number's start ("60" finds 60 QORT, not block 1,364,600); one or two letters from a word's start.
    - **Connections:** every list (minting level, reward shares, names, payments each way with their counts, In common, In Q-Mintership) and the circle; a list with nothing found steps aside; a line says how many rows match ("4 rows match “simon”.", "Nothing matches “smon jmes” exactly; 3 rows are similar."). **Transactions:** the rows read, matches then similar, 50 at a time; the line says how many of how many read ("2 of the 100 transactions read match “simon”. Load older searches older ones too."), and Load older searches on; what waits for a block is searched too.
    - **Reads:** none until something is typed; then names as in the Explorer reads table. It never reads more transactions or payments by itself. While names are still on their way the line says "Looking for “…”…", never "nothing" too early; with no search, no row's text is built.
    - **Checked with real data** against Simon's node: Juan Qortal's Transactions ("simon": his 2 payments with Simon James; "simn jams": the same 2 as similar) and Connections ("simon": 4 rows, YT-LinQ among them because its owner holds "Simon Dixon", now said on the row; "smon jmes": 3 similar; "minter admin": 5 rows; the circle with the 2 accounts found).
    - **Tests:** the matcher (folding, parts, slips, numbers, short words, the Braille character), the rows' text (never a message's content or an encrypted identifier), the field (pause, Enter, Escape, Clear, the link), the Transactions tab (narrowed, kept in the URL, similar under its heading, no extra read, an amount from the link, a message's content never found) and the Connections tab (each list narrowed, counts, a part with nothing steps aside, a slip as similar, an account found by a name it holds, nothing found, Clear), with axe. New screens explorer-connections-search and explorer-transactions-search.
    - **Screenshot check** of the four new screens (explorer-file, explorer-file-list, explorer-connections-search, explorer-transactions-search), every theme and size, dark and light: 160 captures, 0 console errors, 0 sideways overflow, 0 unlabelled buttons, 0 small targets, 0 axe violations. It found one thing to fix: in a phone held sideways (844×390) the viewer was a box with little room for the file; now it is full screen there.
    - Tests 2,259 passed (from 2,187 before items 64–65), 1 expected fail, 8 skipped; build, lint and the theme check pass; the zip is rebuilt after each change.

66. **The whole chain under the search field** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "I also want The whole chain on home page of explorer to be under search bar"). The Explorer's page has Transactions on Qortal right under its search field, before recent explorations, Compare picks and the quick starts (`pages/explorer/ExplorerPage.tsx`; a test pins the order).

67. **The circle split by direction** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "for the circular view I want the transactions to be split into one side transfers in other transfers out (and I want it a bit more transparent, easier to read (threads not overlapping)), and when clicking on each avatar I want to see the list of these transactions, not that users account"). Received from on the left, sent to on the right; each side runs from the top down on its own arc, the most transactions first, with one straight line per avatar, so no line crosses another or passes under an avatar (`rules/explorer/circle.ts`). An account paid both ways is on both sides, each counting that way's transactions. Up to 10 a side (8 on phones), the rest counted under each side; on wide screens the names sit beside the avatars. The other links (reward shares, minting level, names) are chips under the circle. An avatar opens a dialog: what was recorded and its transactions as rows (each opening to its details), with Explore and Compare (`connections/CircleNodeDialog.tsx`). Screens explorer-connections-circle and explorer-connections-circle-busy.

68. **Save File keeps a website's or an app's files as one .zip** (Simon, 2026-10-17, on Q-Support's app of 26 files: "when files are like this, I would like them to be assembled into a .zip and still be downloadable"). On the list of a resource's files, Save File reads each file by its path (4 at a time) and packs them into one ZIP named after the resource ("Q-Support.zip", "AA Maps - beta.zip"; folders kept, files stored as they are, with the writer from Q-Share+). It hands the ZIP to Hub's save prompt as a blob, on desktop and in GO, saying "Packing 12 of 26 files into one ZIP…" while it reads. A file the node can't give stops it and names the file. It isn't offered past 200 MB in all (or for a publish already bigger), and closing the viewer stops it. Checked on Simon's node: Q-Support's app saved as Q-Support.zip, 26 files, 2.9 MB, and `unzip -t` finds no error.

69. **Back opens a page as it was left** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "when going back from the rabbit holes I want the views while going back to open where they were left at"). Each page visit keeps how far down it was scrolled and its views (`hooks/pageVisits.ts`, `hooks/useViewState.ts`):
    - rows and their details opened, the raw response, Show more and Show all, how many transactions show;
    - the payments' side, sort and MINTER filter; the circle's open avatar (its dialog opens again); the activity chart's picks;
    - the names' sort and histories; Compare's and the sets' lists.

    The app's Back, Hub's Back (which now keeps the page's query and state too), the browser's Back and a trail crumb (back to that account as it was left, the visits after it dropped) all bring a page back this way; any other way in is a new visit, at its top. The scroll waits for the page to come in (lists from the session's reads), holds the place until the page settles, and lets go at the first touch, wheel or key. Kept in memory for the session, 60 visits. Checked on Simon's node: clary sage's Transactions with a row open at 500 px, into a transaction and back (the app's Back and the browser's), and through a crumb from another account: the same row open, the same place.

70. **A name's whole history** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "on the names tab for each of the names to have expand to see if there were any changes made to that name (history)"). Under each name's own history, **Its whole history** opens every change since it was registered, by every account that had it: sales, listings, renames and its data, newest first, each saying who did it. The line over it: "Registered 23 Jan 2024 · 2 changes since · 2 owners".
    - Core has no history by name, so it is walked back from the owner (`data/explorer/nameLife.ts`, `rules/explorer/nameLife.ts`): the owner's name transactions (the same read as its Names tab), through a purchase to the seller's, through a rename to the earlier name, down to the registration.
    - The registration found is checked against the time Core keeps in the name's record (through sales and renames).
    - A name renamed away and registered again by someone else is a new registration, with no link to the old one; the walk says where it stops short when it does.
    - Checked on Simon's node: "ᓚᘏᗢ" (registered 18 Jun 2023, no changes since), "AntiWarNews" (listed and sold for 10 QORT, 2 owners), "clary sage" (registered as "ᓚᘏᗢ" on 22 Feb 2023, renamed on 14 Jun 2023).

71. **The Quiz's registrant question** (seen in Simon's screenshots, 2026-10-17). It asked who first registered "ᓚᘏᗢ", which "has changed hands since it was registered", and answered clary sage. But clary sage had renamed "ᓚᘏᗢ" to "clary sage", and another account registered the freed name four days later: it never changed hands. The question now needs the name's current registration to be the one it found (Core keeps the first registration's time through sales and renames).

A name opens its owner's Names tab, scrolled to that name with its whole history open (`explorerNamePath`; the page looks the name up as before). That works wherever a name shows:
    - a transaction's line and its details, a transaction's page, a name's histories;
    - Compare and Connections; the account header's "Opened from the name";
    - Open File's "Published by", and the Quiz.

    Accounts in a transaction's line open too, and so do the Quiz's accounts and its account answers. A transaction row is still one button that opens it, but its words now sit over the button (`TxListRow`), so a press on a name or an account in its line opens that instead. Those links stay out of the Tab order (the opened row has the same ones for the keyboard), and screen readers hear the row once, as its button. Checked on Simon's node: "ᓚᘏᗢ" in clary sage's rename opened The Cat's Names tab on "ᓚᘏᗢ", its history open.

73. **Swipe through an account's tabs** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "for mobile, I would like the ability to swipe (left - right) through the different tabs of Explorer"). On a touch screen, a swipe to the left on an account opens its next tab and a swipe to the right the previous one, in place as the tabs do (`hooks/useSwipeTabs.ts`); the panel follows the finger a little and springs back. A swipe doesn't count:
    - from within 24 px of the screen's sides (the system's Back gesture);
    - on a field, the activity chart or anything that scrolls sideways itself;
    - when the finger moves more up or down than sideways, with two fingers, or with text selected.

    Checked with touch events at 375×812: Overview → Connections → Transactions → Mintership, and back.

74. **Chips say what they mean** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "when there are these minting penalty and so on, can you explain to users on hover what it means (there was an algo when sponsorship was a thing that scanned through the core in search of self-sponsorship… I think you can find this info of what each means on qortal.org)"). Every status chip explains itself on hover and on a tap (a phone shows it at once and keeps it a few seconds); a page's own header chips also take the focus, where screen readers hear it as the chip's description (`components/common/ExplainTip.tsx`, words in `constants/chipExplanations.ts`):
    - the Explorer's chips: a level, MINTER member and In MINTER since, Not in MINTER, Former member, Banned from MINTER, Minter Admin, Dev Admin, Minting now, a minting penalty, a group's Owner and Admin, a Primary name;
    - the MINTER profile's: its level, its status (Nominated, Approved: can join now…) and Join request sent;
    - every nomination's and decision's state ("Collecting approvals", "Too late to approve", "Expired"…), worded from the app's own rules.
    - **A minting penalty**, from Core's source (Qortal's wiki doesn't cover it): Core's self-sponsorship check ran over the whole chain at block 1,092,400 (December 2022) and at blocks 1,611,200 and 1,612,200 (February 2024). It looked for patterns from the days of sponsorship: sponsored accounts passing their rewards on to the same few accounts, sponsees with nothing else, sponsorships given out in bulk, minting levels moved on to other accounts (and, in 2024, QORT sent on in bulk). Each account it matched got −5,000,000 blocks minted, which holds it at level 0. The chip and the Overview's note give the account's own figure: Elon Reeve Musk's is −10,000,000 (matched twice). They don't say "it can't mint": since the level requirement for minting was dropped, Core blocks only founders by the penalty itself.
    - The level, MINTER, Minter Admin and Dev Admin words follow qortal.dev/wiki (Minting, Q-Mintership).

84. **QDN Transactions at a glance** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "in At glance I would like to add the 6th box QDN Transactions which will show the total nr of Tx the user made", then "keep it light as some pcs and users cant handle that large fetches… show like over 1000 tx"). The sixth box counts the account's QDN data transactions (what it published on QDN, as Activity's QDN data chip counts them) in its own transaction list, the one Latest transactions and Activity read anyway: its first page is read here when nothing has yet, nothing more. Once the list reaches its first transaction the count is exact ("172 · Of 205 transactions in all."); until then it is a floor counted in what was read ("860+ · Counted in its newest 1,000 transactions; Activity below reads further back."), and it grows as Activity reads on. Core has no count and no signatures-only search: an exact count of a busy account would take a dozen or more of its half-second searches, so it is never made for this.

96. **Node online** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "in minter profiles you can also show the % of the node online"; then "add it also in the Overview under blocks minted" and "the small i icon and shows more info on hover"). The Minter Profile's facts and the Overview's Blocks minted box show how much of the time the account's node was online, e.g. 95%. An ⓘ beside it explains it on hover, focus or tap (a new `InfoTip`).
    - **How it is worked out.** By Qortal's own count, from Core's source (Block.increaseAccountLevels). An account's blocks minted grows for each reward it is online for, as the minter or the recipient of a reward share: one a block until block 1,508,000, then 1,000 at each reward round (every 1,000th block, for the accounts online in one of the 25 blocks before it). So blocks minted, over the rewards it could have had since its first reward share, is its share online (rules/explorer/onlineShare.ts). Since block 1,945,900 only minting under a MINTER member's reward share counts; the ⓘ says so.
    - **Reads.** Two searches of one row each, about 0.03 s on Simon's node, kept 10 minutes: the oldest reward share, and the newest minting-level move. The tip is the app's cached `chain:tip`.
    - **When it can't be worked out.** A moved minting level (TRANSFER_PRIVS) carries the old account's count with it, so the value is "Can't tell". With no reward share it says "Not minting"; before its first reward round, "Not yet".
    - **Real data.** Checked on Simon's node: Magic Ink at 95% (2,053,093 of 2,153,415 since block 609,585), and an account at 400,000 blocks minted after 418 rounds at 96%.

    Checks: the Overview and Minter Profile captures in two themes, all clean; tests 2,352 passed.

98. **Exchanges** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "add the notification if users were sending QORT to exchanges": TradeOgre `QcZPChiGmwLu3Lrp2mfLSjZ3yHKmtrogRe`, nonkyc.io `QVbi8fvVDeF8jRxBWZvbGJPEtwnnNJFWQh`, in `rules/explorer/exchanges.ts`). Connections opens with a notice when an account paid one, e.g. "Sent 30,297.81457207 QORT to TradeOgre, an exchange, in 10 payments (Jul 2025)." QORT got back from one is a second line. "Show them" lists the payments.
    - **Reads.** `/transactions/payments/between` with both the sender and the recipient: four reads of a few ms each, exact however busy the account is (up to 1,000 a way). On a node without that read, the notice uses the payments the lists read.
    - **Names.** On an exchange's own account the notice says whose it is. Everywhere else those accounts show as "TradeOgre" and "nonkyc.io" instead of an address (`useDisplayName`).

99. **QORT minted, purchased / sold, received / sent** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "add: QORT Minted, and QORT Purchased/QORT Sold (in one), QORT Received/ Sent (in one) summaries. Make sure the QORT Minted is calculated correctly"; then "all fees for transactions go back to the minters, also there are more rewards for Minter Admins and Dev Admins and also there is QORA part"). A row under the Minter Profile's facts, each value with an ⓘ that says how it was worked out.
    - **How QORT minted is worked out.** Core keeps no record of what an account earned by minting, so it is bookkeeping (`rules/explorer/qortBook.ts`): the balance now, less everything the account's transactions moved. That covers payments, transfers, multi-payments and messages in QORT, trade contracts (AT payouts and DEPLOY_AT), name sales, genesis, fees, and asset orders with QORT on one side (settled from the order's state and trades, as Core moves those without a transaction of the account's). What is left reached it without a transaction of its own: minting rewards. Those include everything Simon listed, as Core's Block.distributeBlockReward shows: the transaction fees paid to minters, the extra share for online Minter Admins and Dev Admins, and the legacy QORA holders' share.
    - **Proof.** On Simon's node, 23 accounts that never minted came to exactly 0.00000000. Four are a test fixture of real rows (`__fixtures__/qortBook.json`), covering payments, trades, name sales, asset orders and fees. The node also showed a Core quirk: `/assets/orders/{address}?includeClosed=true` means only closed orders, so each order is read by its id.
    - **Real values.** Simon: 19,306.33 QORT minted. MeadMan: 7,922.41, purchased 499, sold 7,326 (his 101 contracts took 15,427.47 and gave back 8,101.47). Magic Ink: 21,868.60.
    - **Purchased and sold.** Purchased is QORT paid to it by other people's trade contracts; sold is QORT its own contracts paid to buyers (one small read per contract, up to 500). Received / sent is payments, transfers and multi-payments.
    - **Reads.** The sums need the whole history: the list Activity and the Transactions tab share. It reads on by itself up to Activity's first read (1,000 by default). A longer history waits for "Read the rest", which reads 1,000 at a time (an option added to `getAccountTx`): Simon's 15,076 transactions take 8 s. Then the exact balance, the asset orders and the account's own contracts.

    Tests 2,365 passed; the profile captures are clean.

100. **QORT summaries: the notice, colours, and sold for big sellers** (Simon, 2026-10-17: "please display the notification that in order to fetch the ammounts you need to Read the rest of the transactions. also for the ammounts of QORT make the purchased received green and sold sent red", and "why on this account the QORT sold is "?"" for yopilax).
    - **The notice.** When the history is longer than the first read, a framed notice says "To show these amounts, read the rest of its transactions: they add up its whole history, and 1,000+ have been read so far." with a Read the rest button.
    - **Colours.** Purchased and received are green, sold and sent red (readable tones; axe clean in both themes).
    - **The "?".** It came from a cap: "sold" was the sum of the account's own contracts' payouts, one read each, up to 500, and yopilax has 1,154 contracts. Purchased / sold now come from Core's completed trades as the buyer and as the seller (`/crosschain/trades?buyerPublicKey=` / `sellerPublicKey=`, every chain). For MeadMan, sold matches the per-contract sum exactly (7,326). For yopilax it is 1,995 / 139,192, in half a second.
    - **A deliberate exception to the "always a limit" rule.** Each of those two reads has no limit. Core pages this list badly: its limit is at most 100 and applies to each contract version, and its pages repeat and skip rows (for yopilax, pages of 100 gave 431 trades, 100 of them twice, out of 678). Scoped to one account it is about 250 bytes a trade.
    - **Minted is unchanged.** QORT minted still counts every contract payout as income that isn't minting. A payout from another kind of contract (25 QORT for yopilax) is in "paid by other people's contracts" there, not in purchased.

    Tests 2,364 passed; the profile captures are clean.

A view picked by hand while searching is kept. Tests 2,367 passed.

103. **Activity's first reward share** (Simon, 2026-10-18: "there is some kind of bug that shows this indications wrong. please double check and make sure it is showing the milestones correctly", then "also reward share was a thing of the past").
    - **The bug.** The "First reward share (minting)" milestone was the oldest reward share among the transactions read so far, so it moved as more was read. For Stonecrow it showed 15 Jun 2026 with 1,000 read and 16 Mar 2026 with 3,000. The real one is 17 Dec 2021 (block 655,586, a sponsor's share to it).
    - **The fix.** It now comes from the account's oldest reward share in its whole history: the one-row read Node online already makes (`xmintstart:<a>`, which now keeps its signature for the link). The milestone appears once the chart reaches back that far.
    - **The label.** A reward share no longer means minting on its own (since block 1,945,900 only MINTER members' minting counts), so the milestone is now "First reward share". One from before that block is labelled "First reward share (sponsorship era)".
    - **The other milestones** (names, level moves, MINTER events, nominations) are each a real event and were already right.

    Tests 2,371 passed; Overview captures clean.

105. **Minter Admin polls with answers of their own** (Simon, 2026-10-18: "for the ask the minter admins, for the answers, I would like to have it also with ability to change the vote descriptions and add more answers, so the default is Yes, No, Netural. and then MAs can rename it, change color (green, red, neutral) when they are creating the poll").
    - **Asking.** Ask the Minter Admins has an Answers list that starts as Yes, No and Neutral. Each answer can be renamed and given a colour (green, red, or neutral grey). Answers can be added (up to six) or removed (down to two). Review shows them as coloured chips, and the same checks run as when reading: different words, 1–40 characters.
    - **The card.** A poll's columns, vote buttons, the vote dialog, "You voted …", the counts and the comment badges all use its own answers and colours.
    - **Data, additive.** An optional `answers` field on the poll, written only when the answers aren't the default (Data contract). Yes, No and Neutral keep their ids when renamed, and added answers are `opt4` to `opt6`. A poll without `answers`, and every earlier vote, reads exactly as before. A vote for an answer its poll doesn't have doesn't count, and the vote write refuses one before Hub opens. An older copy of the app shows a poll with its own answers as Yes, No and Neutral, and can't count votes for added answers.
    - **Checks.** Tests 2,378 passed. A new screenshot screen (`decisions-ask-answers`) and the poll screens give 30 captures, all clean.

112. **Notes and comments for the Minter Admins only** (Simon, 2026-10-18: "when Minter Admins create a decision, there can be a extra description that will be encrypted only to Minter Admins (not visible to non-Minter Admins) Also On any decision Minter Admins can comment and choose if that comment is public or encrypted and visible only to Minter Admins").
    - **The note.** Propose (every kind), Step down and, for a Minter Admin, Propose nomination have an optional "Note for the Minter Admins" (up to 2,000 characters). It goes as one more Hub step once the proposal (and the proposer's approval) is in: three steps and 0.03 QORT for a proposal, two for a nomination, each said before Hub opens; Review shows the note. A note step that fails never undoes the proposal, and says the note can still be added from its card.
    - **On the cards.** Minter Admins see it under the public reason (Decisions, a decision's own page, waiting or decided, Nominations, a nomination's page), in a frame headed "For the Minter Admins only", with who wrote it and when. Its writer can Edit it, and can add one later on their own decision or nomination while it is open ("Add a note for the Minter Admins"). One written before a Minter Admin could read it says so.
    - **Comments.** In every decision's and nomination's thread a Minter Admin picks "Who can read it": Everyone (public, the default) or Minter Admins only, and the hint and Hub step say which ("Encrypted to the 19 Minter Admins, under your name …"); the choice stays after posting, so a follow-up never goes public by surprise. A comment for the Minter Admins sits in the same thread, marked "Minter Admins only" with a lock; a reply to one is one too ("For the Minter Admins only, like the comment it answers."); an edit keeps its comment's kind; it can't be hidden for everyone (only Minter Admins read it). A Minter Admin's count on the toggle includes them; one that wasn't encrypted to them is left out, and said.
    - **Nobody else** reads, sees or counts any of it: no DOCUMENT_PRIVATE search and no DECRYPT_DATA for anyone else (tests check both).
    - **Data, additive.** Two new records (Data contract): `qmp-adminnote-<key>` and `qmp-macomment-<key>-<id>`, DOCUMENT_PRIVATE, encrypted with Hub's own encryption to every current Minter Admin who can sign and the writer, as the polls' records are. A note counts only from its proposer's names (the nominator's, for a nomination), a comment only from current Minter Admins. The original app never reads them.
    - **Reads.** One shared search each (the last 45 days, at most three pages), only for Minter Admins and once cards near the screen; bodies only where needed, decrypted once per version (the polls' reader).
    - Checks: 28 new tests (records and Hub payloads pinned, who counts, the screens); the full suite, 2,410, passes; new screenshot screens decisions-admin-note and decisions-admin-comments.

113. **Nobody asks to be nominated** (Simon, 2026-10-18: "Remove the ability for non-minters to propose themselfes by joining Minter group first (and any referances of that in our q-app) For users that are not in the minter group refer them to read the Q-Minter Guide, as nominators are selecting community members and proposing them to become a minters and not the non minters announcing that they want to mint").

There the guide shows once: "How minting works" ("Open the Q-Minter Guide", the guide's home) stays for those whose hero doesn't lead with it (signed out, nominated, approved). Copy your name / Copy your address, which invited sending them to a nominator, went too.
    - An account that already has a join request (sent before) reads one plain line: "Your account already has an open request to join MINTER. It stays open until a nomination of you is approved, which then makes you a member at once, or until the Minter Admins decline it."
    - The same everywhere: How joining MINTER works, the Explorer's group cards (Join on MINTER says "MINTER is joined by nomination: a minter at level 5 or above nominates you, and the Minter Admins approve."), others' nomination cards ("Requested to join"), and the changelog.
    - Accept your nomination stays: it is the nominee's own yes once someone has nominated them.

114. **Requested to join MINTER Group, in Propose → Decline a join request** (Simon, 2026-10-18: "The Ready to Mint list (rename it to Requested to join MINTER Group, can be moved to the + Propose into Decline a Join Request, so I still want the ability for MAs to decline join requests").

    - The list, renamed, is now the person picker of Propose → Decline a join request: the same rows (level, blocks minted, when they asked, former member), Sort, search, 20 at a time, each row read only once it is shown. A row offers Decline when no nomination of them is open and no decline is waiting (that one says so, with a link). Picking someone shows them with Change and moves focus to the reason; the optional note follows; Review and Hub as before.
    - For Minter Admins only, as Propose is: nominators don't pick candidates from it any more.
    - While the list shows, the dialog is wider and tall (full screen on phones). Its line under the title is "Turn down someone's request to join MINTER."; that they can send a new request later is said once, after the pick, with the cost, which counts a note when one is written (three Hub steps, 0.03 QORT).

115. **The Explorer's detailed parts for Minter Admins, Dev Admins and level 5+ minters; How Explorer Works** (Simon, 2026-10-18: "hide the detailed explorer windows (the activity charts, minted QORT, sold bought, received sent, and entire connections tab, referances to exchanges) only show them to DEV + MA's and lvl5+ minters. Do not mention it is hidden for these users, but for the users that can see all of explorer say that in How to read the explorer (also update it, and rename to How Explorer Works)").
    - **Who.** Minter Admins, MINTER members at level 5 or above and Dev Admins (the admins of the dev group) see them (`rules/explorer/access.ts`, `hooks/useFullExplorer.ts`). For anyone else, signed out included, none of it is read, and nothing says anything is left out.
    - **What.** Connections (the tab, and the Overview's Direct links; a link to the tab opens the Overview), the Overview's activity chart, QORT minted, purchased / sold and received / sent (the Overview's At a glance, and QORT minted on the Minter Profile), exchanges' accounts named as the exchange (wherever a name shows), and with them Compare and the sponsee and funded lists, which show the same links between accounts (their buttons go; a link opens the account it was about); also the Quiz's 13 questions answered in Connections, Settings' Activity row, and the changelog's notes about them (now a section of their own).
    - **How Explorer Works** replaces How to read the Explorer, brought up to date: finding something, an account's tabs (as the viewer has them), more (Compare, Transactions on Qortal, Open File, Minter Admin activity, the Quiz), where it comes from, its limits, and what acts (joining a group, the Minter Profile's actions: the old guide said nothing ever acts). Those who see everything also read "Who sees what": "You see all of the Explorer. Connections, Compare, the activity chart, QORT minted, purchased / sold and received / sent, and exchanges named as such are shown only to Minter Admins, Dev Admins and MINTER members at level 5 or above."
    - **Found on the way:** the Overview's facts card would have remounted when the viewer's access became known; the QORT reads now switch on in place instead. The Transactions fact's "Activity below reads further back" shows only with Activity on the page.
    - **Real data** (the dev server reading the local node, signed out, so the basic view): the Explorer's home has no Compare; How Explorer Works has no Connections, Compare or Who sees what; an account (Stonecrow) has Overview, Transactions, Minter Profile and Names, and an Overview without direct links, chart or QORT facts, its Minting and Latest transactions loading as they near the screen.
    - Checks: 15 new tests (the rule, each kind of viewer, a Dev Admin outside MINTER, redirects, exchange names, the Quiz, Settings, the changelog); new screenshot screens explorer-account-basic, explorer-how-full and explorer-how-basic.
    - **Checks for items 112–115** (with the follow-ups found while checking: a Minter Admins' comment choice kept after posting, the guide once on a newcomer's Home, the decline's cost with a note, Activity mentioned only to those who have it): the full suite, 2,411 tests, passes (the keyboard walk through every Propose form once ran past its 45 s limit under the full suite's load; alone it takes 13 s). The screenshot check of the 19 affected screens, 5 sizes × 4 themes (380 captures, the fixed ones again), is clean: no console errors, no overflow, no unlabelled controls or small targets, no axe violations.

120. **+ Propose's choices as pages, in Propose nomination's style** (Simon, 2026-10-18: "Make all of these "+ Propose" windows in the style of the "+ Propose" Nomination as they are all different on mobile view, and for example removing Minter Admin should have nice avatars, and also already crossed out then Minter Admins whose removal is ongoing. For example in lift a ban there is this overlapping text mobile, and when clicking lift a ban I would like to have a list of the banned accounts at the bottom too. The same with cancel approved invite and others. And these windows could be made nicer as well").
    - **Pages, not dialogs.** Remove a member, Decline a join request, Ban an account, Make a Minter Admin, Remove a Minter Admin, Lift a ban and Cancel an approved invite each open a page (`/propose/<kind>`, `?who=<address>` to start with someone; `pages/decisions/propose/`), with the app's header and bottom bar, as Propose nomination does. Decisions stays lit in the navigation; Back goes to Decisions.
    - **The same parts on every page:** Who (the kind's list to pick from, then the person picked: avatar, name, level, blocks minted, where they stand in MINTER, the full address with Copy, the same check the write runs, Change); Why (a removal, a ban, a decline: the reason, required); How long (a ban); For the Minter Admins (optional, the encrypted note); What happens next (the Hub steps, the approvals needed and the window, what it does once it passes, the fees); and the sticky submit bar with what's missing or the button ("Propose removing Kai Target", "Propose lifting Bram Banned's ban", short on a phone). The button opens the same Hub steps as before (the proposal, your approval, the note; an admin change says first what it would do to every waiting decision's target). Once proposed, the page says so, with its tab in Decisions and Propose another.
    - **A list on each** (`AccountList.tsx`, `PickLists.tsx`): MINTER's members at first (Remove a member, Ban an account, Make a Minter Admin; a dropdown while typing since item 122), the Minter Admins with your own row pointing to Step down (Remove a Minter Admin), the bans in force, newest first, with when, how long and why (Lift a ban), the approved invites (Cancel an approved invite), and who asked to join (Decline a join request, as before). Each row has its avatar, name, level and blocks minted; 20 at a time, searchable by name or address; each row's account read only once it is shown.
    - **Crossed out:** someone a proposal like it already waits for has their name crossed out and a link to that decision instead of the button ("A removal is waiting for approvals"); one this device has just proposed says "Proposed ✓ (confirming…)".
    - **No more native selects:** the overlapping "Banned account" label on phones and the plain system pickers are gone with them.
    - Removed: `ProposeDialog.tsx` and `StaleInvites.tsx`; the Explorer's read-only lint rule now also keeps it from importing the Propose pages.

Every Explorer header's link button (an account, an AT, an unused address, Sponsees, Funded, Compare, a transaction, the Explorer itself) is named "Copy the link" and copies the page on screen: its tab, and the app's own query (filters, search, order), so a link from Transactions with "Oldest first" opens Transactions with "Oldest first" (`CopyShownLinkButton` in `components/common/CopyLinkButton.tsx`). The button under the Connections circle no longer switches to the List view: it opens the accounts as a list under the circle ("See the list", or "N more accounts with less QORT: see the list"; then "Hide the list"; kept for Back). One list per side in the circle's order (Received from beside Sent to on wider screens, as in the circle, then Sent and received), each row numbered, with its avatar in the side's ring colours, its name and its transactions and QORT, opening the same transactions as its avatar. First "In the circle", then a full-width line and "Not in the circle: N accounts with less QORT (fewer transactions)", then the rest, 20 at a time with Show more (each row looks up a name). 127. **Where it belongs: a QDN publish opens in its app** (Simon, 2026-10-19: "when going into the specific transactions, could we have it so that The transaction it answers links to the website or q-app or whatever that transaction refers to, link Q-Tube comment, or like on Torq or whatever it is, please investigate that approach so it opens correctly"; then "Minter Helper had some of the links capability"). A public QDN publish's page has a **Where it belongs** section under Open File: what it is and in which app, and a button that opens it there in a new Hub tab (`OPEN_NEW_TAB`; outside Hub it copies the link), the link written under it. Investigated in the apps' own code (the + copies, Torq, Q-Tube, the published bundles, Hub's link parser) and MA Helper's portfolio links, whose mapping it follows and corrects (Q-Fund's route is `crowdfund/<name>/<id>`; a Q-Share file's id isn't its share's):
    - **Straight from the identifier:** a website or a Q-App itself; Q-Tube videos (the VIDEO opens its `_metadata` record's page), playlists, channels; Torq posts, revs and their replies, super likes; Q-Share shares (the original Q-Share, not Q-Share+: Simon, 2026-10-19, "for q-share links its not q-share+ but just q-share"; a collection, which only Q-Share+ has a page for, opens its publisher's channel in Q-Share); Q-Fund crowdfunds; Q-Support issues; Q-Shop stores, their data and catalogues; Q-Blog blogs and posts; Q-Music songs and playlists; Ear-Bump playlists. Q-Mintership's own records open their page here: a nomination, its support and votes, a comment (the new comment link, item 128), a card of the original app.
    - **With one read as the page opens:** what a Torq reply answers (its body's `location`; the reply itself opens too, in its conversation), a repost's original, whom a follow follows, a Q-Tube super like's video (`notificationInformation`), a Q-Music comment's song; a Q-Share, Q-Fund or Q-Support comment's parent and a crowdfund update's crowdfund (a search for the key they end in, as those apps find them); a review's store; a Q-Share file's share (its shares' file lists).
    - **On "Find …":** a Q-Tube comment only holds a hash of its video's id, and a Q-Tube like a hash of the id and channel; a Torq like a salted hash of its post's id. The page hashes the videos (or Torq posts and replies) published before it, newest first, 100 a page, at most 1,000, until one matches (SHA-256 in plain code, `utils/sha256.ts`, as Web Crypto isn't there in every frame; it reproduces Quitter's app hash `MhNiRYdzkaP9dz` from its name and salt). On mainnet, through the local node: Zen432's three latest Q-Tube comments each found their video on the first page (TheGreaterEurasiaPodcast, Dr. Eric Berg DC, JonahQ), and two latest Torq likes their posts; the dev server showed "Open the video in Q-Tube" for the first and both links for a Torq reply, no errors but a missing avatar's 404.
    - Where nothing can be found (Q-Tube's old comments, a Q-Support file, an Ear-Bump song), the app itself or the publisher's channel. Encrypted resources get nothing, as before.
    Files: `rules/explorer/qdnPlaces.ts` (pure), `data/explorer/qdnPlace.ts` (the reads), `components/explorer/QdnPlaceSection.tsx`. Tests: 9 for the identifiers, 7 for the reads (a body, a search, a scan that stops at its page, nothing after 1,000), 3 for the section (the Torq reply's two links through OPEN_NEW_TAB, Find on a Q-Tube comment, a nomination's page), SHA-256's standard digests; the transaction screens of the screenshot check (60 captures) are clean. Not checked in a test Hub: each app opening at its link (the routes were read in each app's code; Q-Tube's, Torq's and Q-Share+'s match their own links).

128. **A reply tells its comment's author** (Simon, 2026-10-19: "if there is a new reply to the comment I would like to see the notification that there is a reply to your comment"). Hub can only alert on what a published resource's service, identifier and metadata say, never its body, so a reply now says whose comment it answers in its QDN description: the reply marker (Data contract), as Quitter marks its replies.
    - **Hub alerts:** anyone with a registered name gets a rule for replies to their comments (`qmp-replied-<mark>`), and a Minter Admin a second one for replies to their comments for the Minter Admins; the alert ("Someone replied to your comment.") opens the reply where it was posted. Hub skips one's own publishes, and only a new identifier alerts, so an edit doesn't. Members below level 5 can now turn Hub alerts on for this alone; a nominator with no open nominations too. Alerts set up before this get the reply rules on the next visit to Settings, without a dialog when Hub already allows the app (as the support rules), or are told to turn alerts off and on.
    - **Home:** "Since your last visit" lists "Ada replied to your comment" (or "… for the Minter Admins"), when, and Open; more than three, "And N more replies to your comments". One search by the marker since the last visit (two for a Minter Admin), no bodies; "Mark as seen" clears them; one's own replies are left out.
    - **The comment link** `/comments/<name>/<identifier>` finds where a comment was posted (a nomination from its key; a decision's full signature from the queue, then MINTER's decisions until one starts with the key) and opens it there, scrolled to that comment, focused and highlighted. A link that isn't a comment's, or a decision older than the newest 1,000, says so.
    Only replies published from now on are marked; earlier ones don't alert. Tests: the marker (FNV-1a test values, every name the same shape), the writes (a reply marked, one to one's own comment and a top-level comment not, an edit keeps it, a deletion drops it), the rules and their sync (29), Settings (20, including no name, no role, and a nominator with no open nominations), Home (Zoë's reply to Nora, one search by Nora's marker since her last visit), the comment link (a nomination's, a decision's from the start of its signature, not a comment, not found) and the comments going to the linked one. The screenshot check of Home after a visit, Settings' alerts and a thread (80 captures) is clean. Not checked in a test Hub: a real alert arriving, and its link opening the reply.

Compare stays last in the reading order (`connections/ConnectionRow.tsx`). 132. **Connections: the block beside the sentence, the transactions in full** (Simon, 2026-10-20: "in this view the block numver can be moved to the right of the payment text so the view is more compressed, but when expanded I want to see more details like the transactions details and buttons to open the transaction and so on").
