133. **A narrower toggle on phones** (Simon, 2026-10-20: "on mobile the expand button could be better compressed to not take that much of the side"). Under 600 px a connection's evidence toggle shows only the count and its chevron ("4 ⌄"), with less padding; the sentence beside it says what they are, and the button's name still says it all ("Show the 4 payments with Kit Kicked"). Wider screens keep "4 payments ⌄". A CSS breakpoint, so no row adds a screen-size listener (`connections/ConnectionRow.tsx`). On a 390 px phone, Lou Left's row is a line shorter. The Explorer's tests (301) and the full suite pass; the Connections screens (40 captures) are clean, axe included.

134. **A minting level that moved here: the account it came from** (Simon, 2026-10-20, on crowetic's Minter Profile, whose Node online said "Can't tell": "when the minter level was moved from another account can you fetch that another account data for statistics as well, the online % and qort minted and so on"). Core's TRANSFER_PRIVS adds the sender's blocks minted to the recipient's and clears the sender's, but keeps no public record of how many moved, so the two can't be split; the level's history can be followed instead (`getLevelLineage`, `xlineage:<a>`: the moves into the account, newest first, and the moves into those senders before theirs, up to 6 accounts and 3 moves back; per account one search, two one-row searches and a name).
    - **Node online** (Minter Profile and Overview) is now the share of the rewards the level could have had on every account it was on: the account's blocks minted now over each one's rewards from its first reward share to its move (or to now). On mainnet, crowetic: 96.4% (its level came from crowetic-old, block 2 to 1,095,014, then crowetic since block 1,095,373; its own rewards alone would make it 159%). Its ⓘ names the accounts and blocks. Without the accounts it came from it still says "Can't tell"; a level that moved away still does too.
    - **Where its minting level came from**, under the Minter Profile's facts: each account it moved from, linked, when it moved ("Moved its minting level to this account in block 1,095,014, 12 Dec 2022"), the blocks it minted on before ("Blocks 2–1,095,014"), and for viewers who see the Explorer's detailed parts its QORT minted there (worked out from its own balance and transactions, with the fetch icon when its history is long), then "QORT minted together with crowetic-old account" (Simon's wording, 2026-10-20; each account named when there are more), the total, once each is in. Simon checked it in Hub on crowetic (96%; 174,818 QORT minted there; 302,082 together).
    Files: `data/explorer/account.ts` (getLevelMovesIn, getLevelLineage), `rules/explorer/onlineShare.ts` (lineageShare), `components/explorer/useNodeOnline.ts`, `pages/explorer/account/mintership/LevelSources.tsx`; QortMintedFact takes a label and reports its amount. Tests: crowetic's numbers (2,765,639 countable, 96.4%), "Can't tell" without sources or when moved away, and a Minter Profile where Leo Learner moved his level to Avery Admin (the share's ⓘ, the block, the blocks minted there, QORT minted there). The full suite (2,471), build and lint pass. Not checked in a browser (the browser pane stopped answering; Simon tests it in Hub).

135. **A zero balance as Core writes it** (Simon, 2026-10-20, on kitty's Minter Profile, where "QORT minted there" for TeresaTee stayed at "…"). Core writes some amounts as Java's BigDecimal does, with an exponent: TeresaTee's balance is "0E-8", and one atomic unit would be "1E-8". The amount parser took only plain decimals, so the balance read failed, and QORT minted, which doesn't show its section's notice, waited for ever. `parseAtomic` (`rules/explorer/money.ts`) now reads an exponent too (rejecting anything finer than 8 places), which also fixes the Overview's QORT summaries for such accounts; and QORT minted says "Couldn't read it." with Retry when a read it needs fails (`qortSummaries.tsx` mintedFailed and retryMinted). Tests: "0E-8", "1E-8", "5E-7", "1.5E+3", "1E-9" rejected; the account a level came from with an unreadable balance shows Retry and no total. The full suite (2,472), build and lint pass.

136. **The Quiz, by what the viewer sees** (Simon, 2026-10-20: "make sure the Quiz questions are adapted accordingly depending if the user can see all of explorer or is an account that cant see all, so for example cant open connections and so on"). Item 115 kept the 13 kinds of question answered in Connections from viewers who don't see it. Every kind was checked against what such a viewer has; 11 of the 13 can be answered in the tabs everyone sees, so they are now asked of everyone, and each says where to look by the viewer (`where()` in `data/explorer/quiz/shared.ts`, `QuizContext.full`):
    - **Who sponsored an account** (and its sponsor's sponsor; who sponsored the account a level came from): its Transactions tab → Minting → Reward share, Oldest first: the first account that "started a reward share with this account".
    - **How long a reward share ran**: the same list, the block where the sponsor "removed its reward share with this account" less the block where it started it; asked of them only when that pair is one share and one removal (no rounds to untangle).
    - **Where a minting level came from**: its Transactions tab → Minting → Minting level move, "Got its minting level from …".
    - **Where an account's first QORT came from** (also two steps back, and from a block's biggest payer): its Transactions tab → Payments, Oldest first, the first payment it received (the same transaction Connections' In common names: the one that first credited the account).
    - **What one account paid another, in all or how many times**: the payer's Transactions tab → Payments → Payment, searched for the other account (`?q=`), adding up or counting what it sent; asked of them only up to 8 payments.
    - **A name sale's price and seller**: Transactions on Qortal → Name purchase at that block, or the buyer's Transactions tab → Names → Name purchase ("Bought the name … from …").
    - **Asked only of viewers with Connections**, since only it adds them up: who an account paid the most QORT (`topPayee`) and how many accounts a sponsor sponsored (`sponseesCount`).
    The words, the answer and the level are the same for both; only the hints, how it's found and the link differ, and nothing says that anything is left out. Viewers with Connections keep the Connections wording.
    - **Kept on the device, not by account:** a question asked with Connections wording is marked (`full` on the question and its history entry). Opened for a viewer who doesn't see Connections (another account signed in on the same device, or signed out), it is replaced by another question as if it had never been asked (no score, no skip, the streak kept; `replaceDetailedQuestion`), and the history leaves such questions out for them. While the viewer's access is still being read, such a question waits behind "Thinking of a question".
    Checks: the 6 adapted kinds the mock world can make, each for both viewers (same words and answer; the Transactions tab link and no Connections for one, Connections for the other: Sid Sponsor, a 7,234-block share, Olga Old's level, Lou Left's sale); every kind made for a viewer without Connections never mentions Connections, In common, Compare or the QORT facts; the Transactions tab those links open says what the hint quotes (Leo Learner's reward shares, Kai Target's level move) to a level-1 member; the Quiz bar swaps a Connections question for a level-1 member and keeps it for a nominator, with the history to match. Not checked in a test Hub.

137. **The app's avatar at the top left** (Simon, 2026-10-20: "the top left where the logo supposed to be just add the avatar to the left of Q-Mintership text"). The rail's "QM+" placeholder is now the avatar of the `Q-Mintership` name on QDN (the blue Qortal cube Hub shows for the app), left of the name; on the narrow rail it stands alone. It is bundled, not read from QDN: a 96 px WebP of 2.2 kB, inlined by Vite into the main chunk, so it shows with the first paint, with no request and without a node (`src/assets/q-mintership-avatar.webp`, from `THUMBNAIL/Q-Mintership/qortal_avatar`; the `Q-Mintership+` name has no avatar). Decorative (`alt=""`), since the name beside it says the same. Test: the rail shows it before the name, with no "QM+" (`navItems.test.tsx`). Checked in the browser at 1280 and 800 px.

### 1.0.1 (from 2026-10-20)

1.0.0 was published on 2026-10-20, so new work is 1.0.1 (`package.json`, About and What's new say so). Branch `q-mintership-plus/1.0.1`, on top of #26.

138. **The original Admin Board, readable in History** (Simon, 2026-10-20: "can you make it so the history reads them for Minter Admins, and it says that these were proposed befor 4.0.0"). The old encrypted board's cards were listed nowhere and only linked out to the original app.
    - **History → From the original app** has a fourth filter, **Admin Board** (with a lock), for Minter Admins only. It lists the board's cards newest first, 20 at a time. Each row is decrypted as it shows: its title, "About Member 12 · by Admin B · date" (or "Topic · by …") and an "Admin Board" chip. A card that wasn't encrypted to the viewer reads "Not encrypted to you": one posted before they were a Minter Admin, or one sealed for no one.
    - **The words:** "The original Minter Admin board: cards proposed in the original Q-Mintership, encrypted to the Minter Admins of the day and decrypted for you as you read them, read-only." Upstream, `ORIGINAL_WORDS.era` makes it "proposed before Q-Mintership 4.0.0".
    - **A card's page** ("Admin Board card", subtitle "Proposed in the original Q-Mintership. Encrypted to the Minter Admins, read-only.") shows what it said, who it was about and who published it, its chain poll and its comments, decrypted one by one. A reply shows its quote, and a comment not encrypted to the viewer says so. A topic card names no one.
    - **Others:** anyone else, or a Minter Admin a card wasn't encrypted to, sees who posted it and when. Nothing is decrypted for someone who isn't a Minter Admin, and Admin Board searches don't even run for them.
    - **Reads:** see Data contract → Legacy reads. On mainnet on 2026-10-20: 217 cards (24 Dec 2024 to 8 Oct 2026, 12 names) and 720 comments. Decryption is per viewer: Hub decrypts only what was sealed for the signed-in account, and results are cached under the viewer's address, so switching accounts never shows another account's reading.
    - **Files:** `data/legacyAdminBoard.ts` (index, card and comment reads, `parseAdminCard`), `legacy.ts` (`topic`, comments from MAIL_PRIVATE for Admin Board cards), `hooks/useViewerIsAdmin.ts` (`useAdminViewer`), `pages/history/LegacyBoards.tsx`, `LegacyCardPage.tsx`, `parts/LegacyCardItem.tsx`, `parts/legacyWords.ts`.
    - **Mock world:** `e2e/world-legacy.mjs` has a removal card with its poll and three comments (one not encrypted to Avery Admin), a topic, a card posted before Avery was an admin, and the old unsealed card, sealed with the mock's stand-in encryption (`sealFor`).
    - **Tests:** 9 data tests (search, base64 fetch and DECRYPT_DATA, locked kept, per-viewer keys, a Hub failure that isn't a lock, comments, no writes), page tests for History and the card page as an admin and as a learner, and the Ship-as values test for "Proposed before Q-Mintership 4.0.0". Screenshot check of the new screens (`history-admin-board`, `legacy-admin-card`, `legacy-admin-card-locked`) with `legacy-card` and `history`: 100 captures, 0 console errors, 0 overflow, 0 unlabelled buttons, 0 axe violations.
    - **Needs a Hub check as a Minter Admin with real data:** that Hub decrypts the real cards without a dialog, and how long 20 rows take.

139. **Connections shows every payment, and the whole history once read** (Simon, 2026-10-21, on igorcoin's Connections: "igor sent me 495 QORT which is seen in transactions, but is not visible in connections"; then "since I fetched all the accounts transactions in overview, it should show all history in connections and so on").
    - **The cause:** Connections read only the newest 100 payments each way, from `payments/between`. igorcoin has sent 214, so its 495 QORT of May 2025 and 1 QORT of Aug 2025 to Simon were older than that window. On Simon's node, all 266 of igorcoin's payments read in about 40 ms.
    - **Now:**
      - Payments are read 1,000 at a time until Core has no more, up to 10,000 each way; "Check older" adds 1,000 more past that.
      - Asset transfers and multi-payments are read 1,000 at a time, up to 5,000.
      - Compare's "the same accounts in their payments" and the sponsee sets read the same keys, and their words say "up to the newest 10,000".
    - **The whole history:** once the All list (`xtx:<a>:all`) is read to its end (Overview's Read all of it, the QORT summaries, or any account with fewer than 100 transactions), payments, asset transfers and the links search come from it, complete (`data/explorer/fullHistory.ts`). This covers Connections, Overview's Direct links, Compare, the sponsee sets and the Quiz alike. When it reaches its end, or grows after that, those keys are marked stale so the screen reads them again from it.
    - **Tests:** reads of 1,000 and the 10,000 cap, asset transfers' 5,000 cap, the whole history serving payments, transfers and links with no search, and the stale mark once per new length. Hal Helper's 640 payments now read in one go.

140. **Q-Asset trades in Connections and Transactions** (Simon, 2026-10-21: "both connections and transactions is missing Q-Assets sales and buys, as for example I bought 500 QORT of igorcoin").
    - **Why they were missing:** a trade isn't a transaction between two accounts. Each places its own order (CREATE_ASSET_ORDER; the order's id is its signature), and Core matches them and records the trade against both orders. Simon's case on mainnet: his order bought 23 igorcoin for 529 QORT on 29 Jul 2026, filling igorcoin's sell order of 23 Aug 2025.
    - **Reads** (`data/explorer/trades.ts`, `xassettrades:<a>`; `xtrades:` was taken by the Trade Portal summaries):
      - the account's orders, `/assets/orders/{a}`, newest first, 100 a page, up to 5 pages. Core's `includeClosed` and `includeFulfilled` are exact filters (`is_closed = X AND is_fulfilled = Y`), so three reads: closed and fulfilled, closed but not fulfilled, open. The first version read only the first: on igorcoin, 7 of its orders;
      - for each filled order (the newest 200, 4 at a time), its trades (`/assets/order/{id}/trades`) and the other order (`/assets/order/{id}`, for who placed it);
      - the two order transactions, as evidence and for the trade's block (the later order's). `xfills:<id>` per order, kept for the session.
    - **Connections:** an "Asset trades" part (after Names), one finding per account. One trade reads "Bought 23 igorcoin from igorcoin for 529 QORT" or "Sold … to … for …"; several add up, "3 asset trades with X: got …, gave …". Each opens to its orders. On the circle, what came in and what went out both count, so an account traded with sits at the bottom. It is searched, listed in Copy findings ("Asset trades: every filled order (7).") and in "Also checked, nothing found".
    - **Transactions:** an order's row says how it was filled, "Filled: Sold 23 igorcoin to Simon James for 529 QORT · 29 Jul 2026" (and "and N more"); opened, it lists every trade. The tab reads the account's trades once its rows include an order it placed, so the search finds the order by the other account too. Elsewhere (an evidence list, Transactions on Qortal) an order reads its own fills when opened.
    - **Checked on mainnet** through the dev server reading Simon's node, signed out:
      - igorcoin's Transactions, Trading, searched "simon": "Placed an order to trade igorcoin for QORT (23 Aug 2025). Filled: Sold 23 igorcoin to Simon James for 529 QORT · 29 Jul 2026";
      - Simon's, searched "igorcoin": "Filled: Bought 23 igorcoin from igorcoin for 529 QORT";
      - no console errors.

      Connections needs full Explorer access, so it was checked in the tests only.
    - **Mock world:** Pia Promote's sell order of 6 GEMCOIN, filled by Ben Target's buy order for 120 QORT, and Ben's open order nobody filled. The order routes follow Core's exact filters.
    - **Tests:** the data reads (the three order states, fills only for filled orders, both sides, caching), the words (bought, sold, traded, summed, coverage), the finding and the circle split, Ben's and Pia's Connections, the circle, an account with none, and Ben's Transactions row and search.

141. **One Account report on Connections** (Simon, 2026-10-21: "Cores own report, and public chain and QDN facts, I would like it to be a one button, that creates .md file from all the information known on chain about this account and its connections … and when clicking this it is recommended that user reads entire onchain history of that account first").
    - **The button:** Connections' footer had three things: Core's own sponsorship report, the "public chain and QDN facts" note, and Copy findings. They are now one section, "Account report", with one button that opens a dialog:
      - it recommends reading the account's whole history first and has Read it all there (1,000 at a time through the heavy-read limiter, with Stop); once read, it says so with the count;
      - Make the report reads the rest, shows the file and its name (`account-report-<name>-<YYYY-MM-DD>.md`), and offers Save .md (Hub's SAVE_FILE, `text/markdown`, or a download outside Hub) and Copy.
    - **The file** (`rules/explorer/accountReport.ts`, pure) has the same sections, in the same order, every time, so reports of different accounts read alike side by side:
      - a header table: address, primary name, when it was made (UTC) and at which block, how much of the history was read, the app and version, and a link to open the account;
      - the facts note, then: Account (public key, level, blocks minted, balance, MINTER, minting now, names, groups, first seen, its first own transaction, first QORT received); QORT totals (only once the whole history is read); Activity by type (count, first and last); Connections (level moves, reward shares, names bought or sold, asset trades, payments each way as tables, exchanges, accounts sponsored by the same sponsor, and what was checked and found nothing); Core's sponsorship report; What was read; and the transactions behind the connections (block, time, type, from, full signature; the newest 2,000).
      - Every connection names its block, date and full signature, so each can be checked on any node. Times are UTC; `|` in names is escaped so the tables hold. A part that couldn't be read is named under "Couldn't be read", never taken for none. No verdicts: it says what is on chain, nothing more.
    - **The reads** (`data/explorer/accountReport.ts`) use the account page's own keys, so what the page has read costs nothing again. Core's sponsorship report (1 to 5 seconds) is read only when the report is made. The names of the accounts it mentions are read 4 at a time, at most 500. Nothing is written.
    - **Copy findings** on Connections is gone with it (the report has all it had); Compare keeps its own.
    - **Tests:** the builder (sections in order, tables and escaping, UTC, the history not read, connections with full signatures, payments tables, failed parts, the 2,000 cap, an unused address, the file name with accents and `+`), Kai Target's whole report from the tab (Copy, and Save .md through SAVE_FILE with its file name and text), a part that couldn't be read, the capped links line, and Core's report read only when the report is made.
    - **Screenshot check** (items 139–141, Hub 3.0 dark, every size): the first run found 404s on the 11 screens that read asset orders, because `scripts/screens.mjs` sent only some Core paths to the mock, and `/assets/` was left out since the build's own files sit there. Core's two-segment `/assets/<x>/…` paths now go to the mock (a `Repo:` commit). Then:
      - those 11 screens and the made report: 240 captures, 0 console errors, 0 sideways overflow, 0 unlabelled buttons, 0 small targets;
      - the other 10 Explorer screens from the first run: 200 captures, all 0;
      - axe: one moderate `region` node on one capture (a connection opened, 390×844), in 2 of 5 runs of that screen, never on the others. It looks like something passing outside the landmarks while axe runs.

142. **Q-Assets in the Explorer: an Assets tab and a quick list on the Overview** (Simon, 2026-10-21: "a new tab to Explorer called Assets, and it will be an overview of the Q-Assets the account is holding. In between transactions and minter profile, make it super nice visually, with asset avatars and so on, and in overview a quick list of Q-Assets as well, under At a glance"; then he pointed to the Q-Assets app, gitea.qortal.link/crowetic/Q-Assets, for the avatars). Version 1.0.2.
    - **The tab** (`pages/explorer/account/AssetsTab.tsx`, `assets/`), between Transactions and Minter Profile, for every viewer (holdings are public on chain, like names; Simon: "to everyone"):
      - a summary: how many it holds, what the ones that traded for QORT are worth together at their last trades ("7 of 8 have traded for QORT; the rest aren't counted"), how many assets it owns, and how that worth splits (a bar in each asset's colour, the biggest four and the rest, with a key);
      - each asset as a card (the default) or a row, kept on this device: avatar with the owner's on its edge, name, number and owner, how much it holds, its share of all there is (a bar), its last trade price and what the holding is worth at it, and marks (its own asset; Indivisible, only for an asset in whole units; can't be sent);
      - searched by name or number, sorted (worth most first, biggest share, name, oldest asset), 24 at a time; the search and an opened asset are in the URL (`?q=…&asset=<id>`), so Copy the link opens them;
      - the assets it owns and holds none of, in a section of their own;
      - each asset opens its own popup (`assets/AssetDialog.tsx`; Simon: "make sure when clicking on each asset it gets its own popup overview window"): its description as its owner wrote it, the facts (an indivisible one says "Whole units only: it can't be split"; Simon: "make sure to mention if the asset is divisible or indivisible", then "actually just show it if asset is indivisible"), its biggest holders (the first 100, 10 shown; this account marked, "#15 of 29 holders"), its latest trades with QORT both ways round, who bought from whom, and "Find in its transactions" (the Transactions tab searched for the asset's name).
    - **The Overview** (`overview/AssetsSection.tsx`), right under At a glance: the first 6, worth most first, as tiles; each opens the asset's popup right there (the same `?asset=<id>`, `assets/useOpenAsset.ts`), and "All N in Assets" opens the tab. The same reads as the tab, so opening it costs nothing more.
    - **Avatars** (`components/explorer/AssetAvatar.tsx`, `useAssetImage.ts`), as the Q-Assets app publishes them (its `utils/fetchAssetAvatar.ts` and `constants/qdnConstants.ts`): an IMAGE with identifier `asset<id>_<name>_aavatar` under a name of the asset's owner. One search finds every one (23 on mainnet on 2026-10-21; 13 KB). Shown only when the publisher is the owner's primary name, or another name the owner's names list holds; anyone else's image for an asset is never shown. The avatar store (`data/avatars.ts`) now loads IMAGE resources with the same retries as thumbnails. Without one, a coin in a colour of its own (the theme's info blue turned to the asset's hue, so every theme follows) with the name's first letters ("BHC", "BT", "QA") or its emoji. The Q-Assets app's own default and core images (`assetAvatar_default`, `coreAvatar_*` under the name Q-Assets) aren't used: one shared image would make every asset without its own look the same.
    - **Reads** (`data/explorer/assets.ts`), measured on Simon's node:
      - its holdings, `/assets/balances?address=…&excludeZero=true`, 100 a page (1 KB, 10 ms for Simon; 6 KB for igorcoin's 54);
      - Core's asset list without data, `/assets?includeData=false`, 100 a page (93 assets: one read, 22 KB, 2 ms), kept 30 minutes;
      - their last trades with QORT, `/assets/trades/recent`, both ways round, because Core files a trade under the initiating order's have-asset: 2 reads for up to 40 assets (2 KB, 5 ms);
      - the avatar search, kept 30 minutes;
      - on opening an asset: its holders (`/assets/balances?assetid=…&ordering=ASSET_BALANCE_ACCOUNT&reverse=true&limit=100`, 3 KB) and its trades both ways (`/assets/trades/<id>/0` and `/0/<id>`, 10 each, 28 KB).
      The first look at the tab is about 45 KB in 5 reads. Refresh reads the holdings and prices again (`xholdings:`, `xassetprices:`).
    - **What buyers would pay now** (Simon, 2026-10-21, on crowetic's Q-Assets "worth" 49,698,355.34 QORT, "impossible as there is less than 10 million QORT in circulation": "maybe leave that amount at the last trade, but also add realistic sale amount with its own window next to it, think of the best way"). The cause: soak's last trade was 1 soak for 700 QORT, so crowetic's 70,000 counted as 49,000,000 QORT.
      - **The measure:** each holding sold into the open buy orders on chain (`/assets/openorders/0/<id>`, the best price first, the first 100), until it or the buyers run out; the account's own buy orders never count (crowetic's own bids for Q-Asset and seeds are skipped). It can never be more than QORT buyers have put up. Nothing is sold; the orders are only read.
      - **The summary:** its own panel, "Buyers would pay now", right next to "Worth at their last trades" (side by side on phones too), with who would buy what ("2 with buyers for all of it; 1 for part of it; 2 with no buyers now") and **How it adds up**, a window of its own (`assets/SaleDialog.tsx`): asset by asset, what buyers would pay, for how much of it, the best offer, and its worth at its last trade; each opens the asset's own window.
      - **Each card and row:** both values side by side, "At its last trade" and "Buyers would pay now" ("No buyers now", "for 5,000 of 12,345.678"). A new sort: "Buyers pay most first".
      - **Each asset's window:** the last trade with its size ("700 QORT each (1 soak for 700 QORT, 17 May 2026)"), the holding at that price, what buyers would pay now (worked out there when the tab hasn't read it, as from the Overview), and the open orders: what buyers offer (its own aside) and what sellers ask, 5 price levels a side (`/assets/orderbook/<id>/0`).
      - **The Overview's tiles** read no order books (one read per asset is the tab's), so their amount now says "last trade ≈ …".
      - **On mainnet:** crowetic: ≈ 49,698,355.34 QORT at their last trades, ≈ 3,550.62 QORT buyers would pay now (Q-Asset 3,383.62, GBQ 160, QUIPS 7; soak, 💦, seeds and his own NaviQOR and Qorva have no buyers). soak's window: no buyers, sellers ask 1,000 QORT each, and the trades behind the 700 (Micro Grants bought 1 soak from Swisslibertarian; earlier ones at 0.01 QORT each). The reads: one more per held asset, about 1 KB and under 10 ms each.
    - **Words and numbers** (`rules/explorer/assets.ts`): amounts are whole atomic units, never floats; a price is QORT for one unit from the trade's two amounts; the worth is exact to the atomic unit, shown to 2 decimals ("≈ 420.68 QORT"); a share is a ratio, so any holding above none is never "0 %" (Simon's 0.1 soak of 1,000,000,000 showed "0 %" in the first look on mainnet; now "under 0.01 %"). QORT itself is the balance on the Overview, not a Q-Asset.
    - **Checked on mainnet** through the dev server reading Simon's node, signed out:
      - Simon James: 8 Q-Assets, ≈ 50,094.06 QORT at their last trades (7 priced), owns 2 (BHC and BHC Coin; "71 % of 402,042" BHC, never traded for QORT);
      - Q-Asset opened: Jason's description, Simon #15 of 29 holders (Jason 88 %), and the latest trades, among them "Simon James bought 2,370 Q-Asset from Jason for 455.04 QORT, 0.192 QORT each, 16 Apr 2026";
      - no console errors.
      The dev server can't load relative `/arbitrary/…` images, so the avatars showed as coins there: **needs the Hub check** (and GO) to see the real images.
    - **Mock world:** a catalogue (QORT, Legacy-QORA, QORT-from-QORA, GEMCOIN, Bean Token, 🐉, Moonrise No. 1, a 39-character name with a supply of 10,000,000,000, Ben's Tickets), holdings for nine accounts (Ben Target five; Lou Left none), trades that price three of them, the Q-Assets app's avatar identifiers (Pia's GEMCOIN, Sid's 🐉) and Kit Kicked's image for Nora's Bean Token, which must not show. Bean Token, which shared GEMCOIN's id, has its own.
    - **Screenshot check:** `scripts/screens.mjs` now sends every Core asset read to the mock (the build's own `/assets/` files always have a dot in their name). New screens: Ben's Assets as cards and as a list, GEMCOIN opened, GEMCOIN opened from Avery's Overview, Lou Left with none. All four themes at every size:
      - the asset screens and the account's tabs (13 screens): 260 captures, 0 console errors, 0 sideways overflow, 0 unlabelled buttons, 0 axe violations;

### 1.0.3 (from 2026-10-23)

1.0.2 was published on 2026-10-21, so new work is 1.0.3 (`package.json`, About and What's new say so). Branch `q-mintership-plus/1.0.3`.

143. **Page width: Full window on big screens** (crowetic, the main Qortal dev, 2026-10-23: "it would be MUCH nicer if it was responsive/full width. On my 4K screen it is a tiny width and would be a lot nicer if I could make it fit the window"; Simon: "Make the pages use the window's width on big screens, maybe with a Settings choice like 'Page width: comfortable / full window'. Keep the phone layouts as they are").
    - **Before:** every page was a centred column of 680 to 960 px, so on a 3840 px window it filled a fifth of the width.
    - **Settings → Appearance → Page width** (`state/settings.ts` → `pageWidthAtom`, key `page-width`): **Comfortable** (the default, the column as it was; Simon, 2026-10-23: "Keep Comfortable as the default; people pick Full window in Settings") or **Full window**. Read before the first render (`getOnInit`), so a full-window page never opens narrow and jumps wide. Settings itself follows at once, its sections side by side. On a phone, upright or held sideways, the row isn't shown: there it would change nothing.
    - **Full window** (`components/layout/PageBody.tsx`): the page spans the window. Forms keep 680 px (`$form`: Propose, Ask the Minter Admins, Propose nomination): a field across a 4K screen is no easier to fill in. The page code's loading skeleton follows (`AppShell` → `PageFallback`).
    - **Side by side** (`components/layout/flowColumns.ts`): a CSS grid, `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(min(100%, N px), 1fr))`, as many columns as fit, one when only one does. The comfortable column never has room for two, so Comfortable looks as it did, and so do phones. Cards at least 640 px wide (`CARD_COLUMN`, room for their three answer columns): Decisions' sections (by type, the one queue, Waiting for a Minter Admin, Too late to approve, Minter Admin polls; `Section`'s new `flow`, where everything but the cards spans the row) and the open nominations. Sections at least 560 px (`SECTION_COLUMN`): Home's and Settings' sections, History's months (also the Explorer's Minter Profile timelines) and its cards from the original app. With the rail that is two decision cards a row on a 1920 px screen, three at 2560 and five at 3840. The Explorer, Activity, Compare and a transaction's page were already tables, charts and grids that fill whatever width they get; a decision's, a nomination's or a poll's own page is one card across the window.
    - **Phones:** a page under 600 px wide already spans the screen. A phone held sideways (a frame under 500 px tall, the app's landscape rule) keeps its 760 px column: Full window applies only in taller frames (`FULL_WINDOW_MEDIA`, `@media (min-height: 501px)`).
    - **Tests:** `PageBody.test.tsx` (each width in both modes, forms, the media rule, the grid, a flowing Section's cards and spanning notes), Settings (the choice, saved, Settings itself widening at once, no row on a phone upright or sideways). jsdom applies no media queries, so `test/fullWindow.ts` reads the rule from the stylesheet.
    - **Screenshot check:** `scripts/screens.mjs` takes `--sizes` (a `Repo:` commit), and `QMP_PAGE_WIDTH=full` runs every screen in Full window; two new screens, `settings-full` and `decisions-full`. Hub 3.0 dark unless said:
      - **Comfortable against the 1.0.3 bump's build** (28 screens: Home, Decisions, Nominations, History, Activity, Settings, the Explorer's pages, the forms, a decision, a nomination, a poll), the five sizes: the same but for the new Settings row and data that arrived at a different moment (two runs side by side load at different speeds); compared pixel by pixel, then by eye.
      - **Full window at the five sizes:** 140 captures, all 0 (console errors, sideways overflow, unlabelled buttons, axe). At 360, 390, 844×390 and 700 px the same as Comfortable, but for data timing: phones keep their layout.
      - **Full window at 1920×1080, 2560×1440 and 3840×2160** (38 screens, the Home of each kind of viewer, Compare, Names, Sponsees, a comment thread and the moving target among them): 114 captures, all 0. The page is 1,700, 2,340 and 3,620 px wide; decision cards sit 2, 3 and 5 a row; Home and Settings 4 sections a row at 2560; forms stay 680 px, centred.
      - **All four themes** at those sizes on Home, Decisions, Nominations, History, the admin board and Settings: 72 captures, all 0. History again at the five sizes after its original-app cards flowed too: 20 captures, all 0.
      - Decisions' tab bar showed its scroll arrows in captures taken while Nominations was still loading; settled, no arrows at any of the three sizes (checked in the browser).
    - **Checks:** 2,570 tests, lint, typecheck, build, the theme kit. Another session's checks ran at the same time (load average 54 on 16 cores), and a few heavy page tests hit their 15 s timeout; with 6 workers and a 60 s timeout all pass.

### The morning: Simon's guided real actions, before publishing

Real writes on mainnet, one at a time, by Simon, in a test Hub on his own account with Dev Mode → **Zip** → `release/Q-Mintership+.zip` (the exact bundle; it previews without publishing). Before each step, read what the app's dialog says; in Hub's dialog, check the action, the fee and the fields named below; after Hub, check the result before the next step. Decline in Hub if anything differs. Never Reject unless you mean it.

Today (block ≈ 2,758,700) the only MINTER decision waiting is a member's second "Become a Minter Admin": a double you have already signed; it runs out at block 2,760,156. There are no Q-Mintership+ records on mainnet yet.

1. **A comment (harmless).** Decisions → the double's card → Comments (0) → write a short note → Post.
   - App: "Public, under your name Simon James", 0.01 QORT, one Hub step.
   - Hub: `PUBLISH_QDN_RESOURCE`, service `JSON`, name `Simon James`, identifier `qmp-comment-<the first 40 characters of 5YiHUjTE…>-<6 letters or digits>`, fee 0.01.
   - After: "Publishing…", then the comment with its menu once QDN lists it (a minute or two); Comments (1); the same on the decision's own page.
   - Undo: the comment's menu → Delete (0.01 more; it then reads "Deleted").
2. **Propose nomination (only for someone you would nominate anyway).** If another nomination is already open for someone you'd back, Add your support instead (the same Hub step, identifier `qmp-support-<their address>`). Nominations → Ready to mint (or Propose nomination) → pick them → reason (20–600 characters) → Propose.
   - Hub: `PUBLISH_QDN_RESOURCE`, `JSON`, identifier `qmp-nomination-<their address>`, fee 0.01.
   - After: the card under "Waiting for a Minter Admin"; the link the app gives you opens `/nominations/<address>`; in the original app nothing changes (it has no such cards).
   - Undo: none on QDN; it leaves the open list after 10,080 blocks, and nobody is invited until an admin signs.
3. **Considering, then No longer considering (harmless), on the nomination from step 2** (or any nomination waiting for its first signature). Nominations → the card → Considering → optional note → Mark as considering.
   - Hub: `PUBLISH_QDN_RESOURCE`, `JSON`, identifier `qmp-considering-<their address>`, body with `nomineeAddress`, fee 0.01.
   - After: "1 Minter Admin is considering" on the card, the nomination's page and the nominee's Home.
   - Undo: No longer considering publishes the same identifier again with `cleared: true` (0.01).
   - Considering isn't offered on a double, so the waiting double can't be used for this.
4. **A signature (only on a decision you would sign anyway).** The first signature on your nomination from step 2: its card → Sign.
   - App: two Hub steps, 0.02 QORT: the invite, then your own signature.
   - Hub, step 1: `INVITE_TO_GROUP`, `groupId: 694`, `inviteeAddress` theirs, `inviteTime: 864000`, `txGroupId: 694`, fee 0.01.
   - Hub, step 2: `SIGN_TRANSACTION` with the raw approval: `"approval": true`, `pendingSignature` starting with the invite's signature, fee 0.01.
   - After: "Signed ✓ (confirming…)", then 1 of 8 once it is in a block; the same decision in the original app's view of pending approvals; Home's counts.
   - Undo: none. Withdraw my signature is a rejection on chain; the invite simply runs out after about 8.5 days if nobody else signs.
5. **Optional, a Minter Admin poll (only for a question you want to ask the admins).** Decisions → + Propose → Ask the Minter Admins (a page) → question, duration → Ask the Minter Admins → Publish.
   - Hub: `PUBLISH_QDN_RESOURCE`, service `DOCUMENT_PRIVATE`, identifier `qmp-adminpoll-<8 letters or digits>`, encrypted (Hub shows it is encrypted), fee 0.01.
   - After: the poll on Decisions under Minter Admin polls, readable by the other Minter Admins in their own Hub; Vote (one more step, `qmp-adminvote-<id>`).
   - Undo: none; it runs out at its end.
6. **Reject and Decline a join request:** only if you mean them. A rejection counts against a decision exactly as a signature counts for it; a decline removes someone from Ready to mint once enough Minter Admins sign.

Report anything that differs from these words before going on; the app is ready to publish once these match.

### Open questions for Simon (night 3)

From building tonight's work; the app works either way.

1. **Removal doubles:** a removal that is a double offers Reject only (there's nothing to remove), while an addition that is a double keeps a secondary Sign. Keep?
2. **Rule (a) while a removal of the same admin waits:** an addition of someone who is a Minter Admin is still marked a double, even if a waiting removal of them could pass first. Keep, or don't mark it then?
3. **Rule (c):** the newer copy is the double even when it has more signatures than the older one. Keep, or call the one with more signatures the real one?
4. **Activity and decided doubles:** a double that was signed anyway and approved, or rejected, still counts as eligible (never as a miss) for admins who left it alone. Leave those out too?
5. **Decline a join request:** offered on Ready to mint rows without an open nomination, and in Propose. Also on rows with an open nomination, or on the person page? And should Home tell someone whose join request was declined (their person page says so)? Activity's pulse still counts declines among "removed".
6. **Nominators' alerts:** to keep alerts on between nominations, the last support rule stays until a new nomination's rule replaces it, so a later support for that person still alerts. OK, or a per-device "alerts wanted" flag instead?
7. **Supporters:** Since your last visit outcomes and support alerts are for nominators only. Supporters too?
8. **Home's target line** shows when the signatures needed moved, not when only the admin seats moved (Decisions says both). OK?
9. **Wording to check:** "Your nomination won't go through this time", "Your account and your level stay as they are", "Level 5+ is suggested for Minter Admins, and you're level 2. You can still propose yourself.", "As things stand, if this one passes, nothing changes".
10. **Minter Admin polls:** closing a poll early or correcting its question isn't offered (republishing would replace it for everyone). Wanted? And an opt-in Hub alert when a poll is published (it could only say one was published)?
11. **Refresh buttons** stay enabled while they read (a press during a read is ignored), so focus stays on them. OK?

1. **Bottom bar:** "Nominees" stays.
2. **Classic light colours:** keep the darker shades (teal `#186d64`, gold `#8b6016`).
3. **Stale invites:** stale means approved more than 30 days ago, as built. Home's tile counts every approved invite not used, and says so.
4. **Proposals chain the proposer's signature,** as the first signature does. Built (see Done).
5. **Reasons** for kicks and bans stay required.
6. **Sign on unconfirmed decisions:** proven on the testnet (night 2): an invite and its first approval were confirmed in the same block (17).

8. **"Other transactions waiting for Minter Admins":** the wording stays.

11. **History:** as built.
12. **Ready to mint:** nominators find quality users and reach out; newcomers can show they're ready with a join request, after a readiness checklist. Built, in place of the generic "find a nominator" help.

14. **History clean-up:** done, and the old `q-mintership-plus/rewrite-phase-1` branch is deleted from GitHub.

### Open questions of 2026-10-13: answered and built (night 2)

1. **"Propose cancelling all" adds your signature to each cancellation:** yes. Two Hub steps per invite; the whole run's fees are checked first.
2. **The two-fee check before a proposal:** right, and it stays quiet unless the balance can't pay.
3. **Cancel an approved invite** also lists invites of people already in MINTER, after the unused ones, marked "already a member".
4. **Accept's check** now reads "Your join request is already in: you're ready. Nothing more to send.", however the request was sent.

6. **"Find a nominator"** is gone; tests pin that nothing offers it.
7. **Ready to mint marks former members:** one history read per row on screen, kept 10 minutes.
8. **Search on Ready to mint:** by address, or by name through one cached name search.

### Open questions for Simon (night 2)

From building tonight's work; each is a choice, the app works either way.

**Signing and the target**

1. "I've checked these" is also required when an admin change only raises the target. Keep it, or ask only when the target drops?
2. In Sign several, an admin change with an impact needs the same tick. Keep it, or leave such changes out of a bulk run with a reason?
3. Only Decisions says "The target changed". Should Home's admin section show a short line linking to it?
4. Activity counts participation as signatures ÷ eligible, so a deliberate rejection lowers it. Count any answer as taking part?
5. "Waiting for your signature", the Decisions badge and Sign several leave out decisions you rejected (each can still be signed from its card). Right? And should "Hide what I signed" become "Hide what I answered"?
6. Considering is offered only to admins who haven't answered. Should one who rejected be able to say they're reconsidering?
7. The nominee's Home shows how many Minter Admins have rejected so far and how many are considering. Is the wording gentle enough?

**Admin roles and links**

8. Become a Minter Admin is offered to every member, level 0 included. Should it need a level, such as 5+?
9. Step down is on an admin's Home, their own page and in Propose. Right, or only the person page?
10. The link a nominator copies after Propose nomination opens the nominee's person page. Open `/nominations/<address>` instead?
11. Should History's decided events link to their decision page too?

**Comments**

12. Add a "Muted names" list with Unmute to Settings? Today you unmute from a muted comment's own menu.
13. Comment counts include deleted and hidden comments. Count only readable ones? Every body would have to be read, so counts would no longer be one shared search.
14. A just-posted comment shows "Publishing…" with no menu until QDN lists it, so it can't be edited at once. OK?
15. The shared count search covers 45 days and up to 300 comments; older items search per card once on screen. Are those the right numbers?

**Nominations and wording**

16. "Propose nomination" (sentence case), or "Propose Nomination"? It is one constant. And should descriptive uses ("You can nominate new minters") change too?
17. The floating "Propose nomination" button is about 200 px wide on phones. Make it icon-only, with a label for screen readers, on narrow screens?
18. Others' nomination cards say "Ready to mint" for an open join request; Decisions keeps "Hasn't accepted yet: after approval they still need to press Join now." when there is none. OK?
19. Propose cancelling all stops before it starts if the balance can't pay every fee. OK, or run as many as it can pay?
20. The Nominations search text is kept when switching between open nominations and Ready to mint. Clear it instead?
21. A former member whose ban ended or was lifted reads "removed from MINTER". Should a ban get its own word?

**Notifications**

22. The Hub evidence comes from GO's source. Please confirm the desktop Hub people use also has the NOTIFICATION_* requests (the app checks with SHOW_ACTIONS either way, and says "Your Hub doesn't offer app alerts" without them).
23. Alerts for signatures, approvals or a new decision would need Core's notifications socket to send group-approval events. Raise it as a Core track?
24. Nominees without a registered name get no Hub alert, as Hub only listens for named accounts. Accept it, or ask the Hub team?
25. Should nominators get "Since your last visit" items for the nominations they made (approved, expired), and an alert when someone supports one?
26. The "Since your last visit" panel sits above Home's status hero. Keep it there, or put it under?

**Look**

27. People named inside sentences ("Proposed by Admin B") are links in the sentence's own colour, underlined only on hover. Underline them, or use primary?
28. The signature ring's "of 8" is 11–13 px; the ring has a full label for screen readers. Keep it, or enlarge it on 56 px rings?

### Core: what an explorer needs that Core doesn't give (proposals)

Found while building the Explorer; none blocks it. Each would be a read-only endpoint the app can feature-detect (a 404 means it falls back to what it does today).

1. **TRANSFER_PRIVS JSON has no `recipient`.** `TransferPrivsTransactionData` isn't in `TransactionData`'s `@XmlSeeAlso`, so its own fields aren't written; the app reads the recipient from `/transactions/signature/{sig}/raw` (bytes 112–136).
2. **No name history.** `getTransactionsInvolvingName` exists inside Core; a `GET /names/{name}/history` would show every owner of a name in one read. The Names tab walks it back now (item 70: one read per owner), but can't see a name's earlier registrations (one renamed away and registered again), which such an endpoint would.
3. **No counterparty summary.** `GET /addresses/{a}/counterparties?txType=…` (sent and received counts and amounts per account, first and last block) would replace paging through payments, make overlaps exact, and mark busy accounts for free.
4. **No counts.** A count for `/transactions/search` (as `/chat/messages/count` has) would turn the 100+/500+/1,000+ payer test into one read.
5. **Sponsor and sponsees.** `/addresses/sponsorship/{a}/sponsor` takes an arbitrary row (no ORDER BY) and the report lists sponsees' names, not addresses; its `nonRegisteredCount` is sponsees minus names, which goes negative with several names. A sponsees list with addresses, dates and removals would serve explorers directly.
6. **First funding.** Following an account's first reference works for most accounts but not after a fee-less first transaction; `GET /addresses/{a}/origin`, and the reverse ("accounts first funded by X"), would make the funder views exact.
7. **No minting history per account.** Core keeps no record of which reward batches an account was online for, and `/blocks/onlineaccounts/{h}` answers an empty list on mainnet today; a per-account history would allow "minted in the same periods" comparisons.
8. **Primary names in bulk:** `getPrimaryNamesByOwners` exists inside Core; one endpoint for many addresses would save a read per row.
9. **Searches that cost more than they should:** `/transactions/search` with an `address` and a block range (`startBlock`, `blockLimit`) took 4 min 44 s on Simon's account where the same search without the range took 0.4 s (the range is filtered on the Transactions table before the account's own rows; the Explorer never sends it); `searchsimple` with several `name=` and no service scans everything (0.5 s for 2 names; one name is 1–10 ms); `/transactions/address/{a}` has no limit by default; `/addresses/rewardshares?involving=` takes 0.4–1.2 s where `recipients=` takes 40 ms.

### Next-pass ideas

- Settings sync to QDN (like Q-Share+'s `settingsQdn.ts`); the settings, the last-visit snapshot, the seen target and muted names stay in the browser for now.
- Hub's `SHOW_ACTIONS` feature detection, to switch to a GROUP_APPROVAL action if Hub adds one.
- The level spread on Activity reads each member's account on request; a Core endpoint for levels of a group's members would make it one call.
- A Core issue for the fresh-chain start-up (the empty "latest signatures" batch on a chain with no QDN resources), with `testnet/lib/PrimeChain.java` as the workaround it would retire.
- Opt-in Hub alerts for comments and Considering ("someone commented on your nomination"), as both are QDN records.
- Theme kit, for every app: the light themes' off-state switch track is about 2.7:1 (a control's boundary needs 3:1); captions and field helper text are 12 px where docs/DESIGN.md asks 14 px on phones; Hub 3.0 light's success, warning and info colours are 2.4–3.5:1 on paper (this app only uses them beside words); a kit-level focus ring, as docs/DESIGN.md describes.
- The page tests are sensitive to a heavily loaded machine: a vitest `maxWorkers` limit, or longer timeouts on the heaviest page files, for overnight runs.
- Cleanup: DecisionCard's `highlighted` prop and `decisionCardId` anchor are unused now that `?d=` links redirect.
- Explorer: a Hub Dev Mode check (round 4) with real data at the five sizes, counting reads per screen in Hub (a first look on 2026-10-18, as a Minter Admin: complete); and in GO on a phone.
- Explorer: the same `/groups/member/{a}` is read twice when an account opens (the MINTER membership and the Explorer's groups use separate keys); one shared loader would save a read.
- Explorer, Transactions: a window far back in a busy account's history takes about a dozen searches to find (several seconds on Simon's account, said as "Still checking"); a count of an account's transactions from Core (Core proposal 4) would let the app jump there in one or two.
- Explorer: a few list buttons (the landing page's short lists, a transaction's fields) still drop keyboard focus when pressed; the funded page's checks aren't stopped when the page closes (at most 50 small reads).
- Explorer: History's "Find a person" could use the Explorer's field (suggestions, exact lookup), still opening the person page.
- Explorer: the screenshot check flags buttons under 40 px; DESIGN.md asks 44.
- Open File: Markdown rendered (not only as text), a font's sample, a file inside a ZIP opened, Office documents previewed (would need a library such as mammoth or SheetJS, lazily loaded).
- Open File: Core's `GET /arbitrary/…` takes a byte range's end as given, past the file's end too, and promises bytes it hasn't got; a Core fix would let the first read take the file's head and size at once (now one byte, then the head).
- The search on the global Transactions on Qortal page could match "similar" too (its "Only rows with" is exact).
- Explorer, Connections circle: between 600 and 900 px wide with 10 accounts on a side, the names near the top of a side run under the next avatar. Fewer on a side at that width, or names that alternate sides, would clear them.
